INFRASTRUCTURAL MEDICINE Health, Exposure Architecture, Biological Embodiment, Slow Harm, Care, and Structural

Causation

FIRST EDITION — EMPIRICAL FIELD MANUSCRIPT SignalRupture

(SR)

·

2026

Preface Modern medicine is extraordinarily capable at acute rescue and increasingly strained

at

explaining

why

populations

remain

exposed

to

conditions

that

repeatedly

generate

disease.

Infrastructural

Medicine

begins

at

that

gap.

It

asks

what

happens

when

the

unit

of

analysis

is

expanded

from

the

patient

and

the

pathogen

to

the

food

system,

water

system,

housing

system,

labor

system,

administrative

system,

pharmaceutical

supply

chain,

digital

environment,

and

healthcare

architecture

within

which

biology

is

continuously

produced.

This field does not deny genetics, pathogens, individual behavior, clinical medicine,

or

personal

agency.

It

rejects

a

narrower

error:

treating

those

levels

as

if

they

arise

outside

the

material

systems

that

distribute

exposure,

constraint,

recovery

capacity,

diagnosis,

and

treatment.

The

central

SR

proposition is that health is embodied infrastructure. A body records what a system

repeatedly

permits,

withholds,

concentrates,

delays,

normalizes,

and

makes

difficult

to

escape.

The manuscript consolidates the health-related SignalRupture corpus into a first-edition

field

architecture.

Earlier

SR

papers

are

treated

as

conceptual

source

material

rather

than

as

automatically

verified

empirical

authority.

Where

an

earlier

claim

outruns

the

evidence,

the

field

formulation

is

narrowed.

Where

the

evidence

is

strong,

it

is

retained.

Where

causation

remains

unresolved,

the

manuscript

explicitly

distinguishes

association,

mechanism,

mediation,

and

causal

inference.

This architecture does not replace clinical medicine, public health, epidemiology, environmental

health,

occupational

medicine,

exposomics,

or

social

medicine.

It

proposes

a

distinct

causal

map

for

testing

how

upstream

systems

produce,

route,

persist,

or

modify

exposures

before

disease

becomes

clinically

visible.

Abstract Infrastructural Medicine is proposed as a transdisciplinary field for studying how

material,

administrative,

economic,

environmental,

informational,

and

healthcare

infrastructures

become

biologically

embodied

across

time.

The

field integrates SR concepts of Slow Harm, Systemic Erosion, the Harm Stack,

pathologized

infrastructure,

visibility

lag,

administrative

harm,

constrained

choice,

and

infrastructural

causation.

It

formalizes

a

causal

sequence

in

which

infrastructure

shapes

exposure

and

constraint;

exposure

interacts

with

susceptibility

and

behavior;

repeated

load

becomes

biological

incorporation;

accumulated

injury

alters

disease

probability;

and

healthcare

systems

then

detect,

classify,

treat,

delay,

or

misclassify

the

resulting

condition.

The

framework

is

deliberately

non-reductionist:

disease

is

not

assigned

to

infrastructure

merely

because

infrastructure

is

present.

Instead,

the

field

requires

explicit

causal

pathways,

counterfactuals,

competing

explanations,

temporal

ordering,

and

uncertainty.

The

empirical

program

proposed

here

spans

mental

health,

ADHD,

nutrition,

food

systems,

drinking

water,

cancer,

chronic

stress,

pharmaceutical

access,

drug

shortages,

public-health

failure,

administrative

burden,

healthcare

access,

and

cumulative

multi-system

exposure.

The

central

research

problem

is

the

Human–Infrastructure

Attribution

Problem:

how

much

observed

morbidity

is

best

explained

by

individual-level

variation,

upstream

infrastructure,

interaction between the two, or unresolved causes? The manuscript proposes measurement

families

and

falsification

criteria

rather

than

a

predetermined

percentage.

Infrastructural

Medicine

therefore

reframes

prevention:

not

as

the

demand

that

individuals

privately

compensate

for

public

exposure,

but

as

the

redesign

of

systems

that

systematically

distribute

avoidable

biological

load.

Field Declaration Infrastructural Medicine studies the production, distribution, accumulation, recognition,

and

treatment

of

biological

harm

through

infrastructure.

Its

object

is

not

merely

the

healthcare

system.

Its

object

is

the

entire

causal

environment

through

which

bodies

are

exposed,

constrained,

protected,

diagnosed,

and

repaired.

Infrastructure → Exposure → Constraint/Behavior → Biological Incorporation

→

Slow

Harm

→

Disease

→

Detection

→

Treatment

→

Outcome

Outcome → Administrative/Economic State → Future Exposure The field is organized around three propositions and an explicit incremental-validity requirement.

These

are

not

declarations

of

settled

empirical

law.

They

define

the

empirical burden that must be met for Infrastructural Medicine to remain a distinct field rather

than

a

relabeling

of

social

epidemiology,

environmental

health,

public

health,

or

exposomics.

Embodiment Proposition Infrastructure becomes medically consequential when it changes exposure, constraint,

protection,

or

recovery

in

ways

that

can

accumulate

across

time

and

become

embodied.

Slow

Harm

names

the

temporal

process;

the

Eroded

Subject

names

the

resulting

state

of

reduced

buffering

or

increased

vulnerability.

Neither

term

is

a

diagnosis.

Infrastructure → Exposure / Constraint → Slow Harm → Eroded Subject Manifestation Proposition Disease expression remains disease-specific. Biology, genetics, development, infection,

behavior,

stochastic

processes,

treatment,

and

protective

conditions

can

modify

or

dominate

a

pathway.

SR

therefore

asks

whether

an

eroded

state

changes

the

probability,

severity,

persistence,

detection,

or

recovery

trajectory

of

illness

rather

than

claiming

that

infrastructure

substitutes

for

pathophysiology.

Illnessᵢ(t) = f[Biologyᵢ, Erosionᵢ(t), Exposureᵢ(t), Protectionᵢ(t), Careᵢ(t), Chanceᵢ(t)]

Responsibility Proposition The location where illness becomes visible is not necessarily the location where

its

modifiable

conditions

were

controlled.

Responsibility

analysis

begins

only

after

a

causal

pathway

is

supported

and

then

examines

control,

foreseeability,

capacity,

preventability,

viable

alternatives,

and

burden

distribution.

It

does

not

infer

legal

liability

from

structural

association.

Responsibility ≠ Proximity to Illness Together these propositions define the field more tightly than a catalogue of health

domains.

Food,

water,

housing,

labor,

pharmaceuticals,

mental

health,

cancer,

digital

systems,

and

other

chapters

are

application

domains

in

which

the

propositions

can

be

tested;

their

inclusion

does

not

mean

that

SR

has

already

demonstrated

an

infrastructural

explanation

for

every

outcome

discussed.

Accordingly, the framework sits upstream and downstream of conventional medicine

simultaneously.

Upstream,

it

studies

why

harmful

exposures

and

constrained choices are patterned. Downstream, it studies how institutional detection,

waiting,

eligibility,

pharmaceutical

access,

and

administrative

friction

modify

disease

trajectories

after

illness

has

already

emerged.

1. Intellectual Position and Literature The field has strong neighbors rather than an empty intellectual landscape. Social

epidemiology,

environmental

health,

occupational

medicine,

public

health,

health-services

research,

social

determinants

of

health,

commercial

determinants

of

health,

exposomics,

systems

medicine,

nutritional

psychiatry,

implementation

science,

and

political

economy

already

document

parts

of

the

causal

terrain.

The

novelty

claimed

here

is

architectural:

SR

joins

these

levels

through

a

common

vocabulary

of

infrastructure,

exposure,

slow

harm,

visibility,

conversion,

and

repair.

The World Health Organization defines social determinants as the conditions in

which

people

are

born,

grow,

live,

work

and

age,

together

with

access

to

power,

money

and

resources.

Its

2025

world

report

emphasizes

that

health

inequities

are

produced

and

reproduced

through

these

wider

conditions.

Infrastructural

Medicine

accepts

this

foundation

but

sharpens

the

analytic

question: which concrete systems transmit those determinants into repeated exposures,

constraints,

delays,

and

biological

loads,

and

through

which

pathways?

Commercial-determinants scholarship similarly shows that business practices can

shape

physical,

social,

and

cultural

environments.

SR

extends

this

by

examining

the

infrastructures

through

which

commercial

decisions

become

difficult

for

individuals

to

avoid:

product

formulation,

retail

geography,

insurance

design,

formularies,

platform

incentives,

supply

chains,

housing

markets,

and

environmental

externalities.

The field does not claim that every social gradient proves infrastructure caused

an

individual

disease.

Population

pattern

is

evidence

of

structured

distribution,

not

sufficient

proof

of

a

specific

causal

pathway.

The

research

burden

remains

disease-specific

and

pathway-specific.

2. The SR Causal Standard Field-boundary requirement. A pathway can be structurally real without being uniquely Infrastructural

Medicine.

The

field's

distinct

scientific

claim

requires

comparison

against

established

baselines. For a specified health outcome Y at horizon h: M0: Y(t+h) = f(individual risk, socioeconomic factors, measured exposure, clinical variables,

demographics)

MIM: Y(t+h) = M0 + infrastructure source architecture + exposure routing + persistence +

substitutability

+

recovery

conditions A stronger field claim requires Performance(MIM) > Performance(M0) on held-out, prospective,

quasi-experimental,

or

otherwise

defensible

comparative

data.

If

infrastructure

variables

do

not

improve

explanation,

prediction,

or

intervention

targeting

beyond

existing

models,

the

field

should

be

narrowed. Infrastructural Medicine separates five evidentiary statements that are often collapsed

into

one:

a

variable

is

associated

with

disease;

an

exposure

precedes

disease;

a

plausible

mechanism

exists;

the

exposure

causally

changes

risk;

and

an

upstream

infrastructure

causally

changes

the

exposure.

Each

statement

requires

different

evidence.

Association ≠ Mechanism ≠ Mediation ≠ Causation ≠ Upstream Causation A strong infrastructural claim therefore requires a chain. If neighborhood food

access

is

proposed

as

an

upstream

cause

of

metabolic

disease,

the

analysis

must

test

whether

the

infrastructure

changes

actual

dietary

exposure,

whether

dietary

exposure

changes

relevant

biological

pathways,

whether

alternative

explanations

are

adequately

controlled,

and

whether

changing

the

infrastructure

changes

outcomes

or

intermediate

endpoints.

This standard protects SR from a common failure of structural critique: moving

directly

from

moral

plausibility

to

causal

certainty.

Infrastructure

can

matter

profoundly

without

explaining

everything.

3. The Human–Infrastructure Attribution Problem Yᵈ = Hᵈ + Iᵈ + Xᵈ + Uᵈ For disease domain d, Yᵈ denotes the modeled burden under study; Hᵈ is the contribution

attributable

primarily

to

individual

or

endogenous

variation

under

the

specified

counterfactual;

Iᵈ

is

the

contribution

attributable

to

infrastructural

exposure;

Xᵈ

is

interaction

between

individual

susceptibility

and

infrastructure;

and

Uᵈ

is

unresolved

attribution.

These

terms

are

conceptual

partitions,

not

automatically

additive

epidemiological

fractions.

Real

causal

components

can

overlap.

No fixed human-versus-infrastructure percentage is assumed in advance; an estimate

such

as

'70%

infrastructure.'

The

empirical

task

is

to

estimate

pathway-specific

effects,

uncertainty,

mediation,

and

interaction.

A

finding

that

infrastructure

explains

little

in

a

domain

would

count

against

a

broad

SR

claim.

A

field

that

cannot

lose

is

not

a

scientific

field.

3A. EMPIRICAL FIELD STATUS — CANADIAN MULTI-SYSTEM TEST Infrastructural Medicine is tested here with current Canadian evidence rather than left as a

prospective

declaration.

The

tests

are

selected

because

they

expose

different

links

in

the

proposed

chain:

infrastructure-to-exposure,

exposure-to-health,

built-environment

modification

of

exposure,

restoration

of

infrastructure,

and

recognition

lag.

They

do

not

establish

that

all

disease

is

infrastructural. 3A.1 Core empirical chain Source Infrastructure → Exposure Distribution → Persistence/Recovery → Biological or Functional

Effect

→

Recognition/Care The empirical burden increases from left to right. A drinking-water advisory can directly show

infrastructure-to-exposure

risk

without

establishing

disease

incidence.

Air-pollution

health-impact

assessment

can

connect

population

exposure

to

attributable

health

burden.

The

2021

British

Columbia

heat

dome

provides

a

sharper

built-environment

case

because

indoor

thermal

conditions,

cooling

access,

social

isolation,

and

mortality

were

observed

in

the

same

event. 3A.2 Test A — Ambient air pollution as distributed infrastructural exposure Health Canada's 2021 national health-impact assessment estimated that above-background

PM2.5,

nitrogen

dioxide,

and

ozone

exposure

contributed

to

approximately

15,300

premature

deaths

per

year

in

Canada,

along

with

about

2.7

million

asthma

symptom

days,

35

million

acute

respiratory

symptom

days,

and

8,100

emergency-room

visits.

The

assessment

estimated

geographically

varying

mortality

rates

that

generally

tracked

spatial

air-pollution

patterns

(Health

Canada,

2021). Result: strong support for the exposure-distribution and biological-consequence portions of

the

field.

Air

pollution

is

not

merely

an

individual

'choice'

exposure;

transport,

energy,

industry,

land

use,

combustion

sources,

atmospheric

processes,

and

urban

form

influence

ambient

concentrations.

However,

the

Health

Canada

assessment

does

not

by

itself

identify

which

infrastructure

configuration

is

causally

responsible

for

each

attributable

death.

The

infrastructure-specific

contribution

must

therefore

be

modeled

at

source

and

intervention

level. 3A.3 Test B — Extreme heat, housing, cooling, and mortality British Columbia's 2021 extreme heat event produced 619 coroner-identified heat-related deaths.

The

provincial

death-review

findings

reported

that

98%

of

deaths

occurred

indoors,

67%

of

decedents

were

aged

70

or

older,

56%

lived

alone,

socially

and

materially deprived neighbourhoods were overrepresented, and most decedents were in homes

without

adequate

cooling

such

as

air

conditioning

or

fans.

The

review

also

found

a

lag

between

heat

alerts

and

public

response

(BC

Coroners

Service

/

Province

of

British

Columbia,

2022). Result: strong event-level evidence for infrastructural routing and protection. The external

meteorological

hazard

was

shared

broadly,

but

fatal

exposure

was

strongly

concentrated

in

indoor

residential

environments

with

inadequate

cooling

and

among

people

with

higher

susceptibility

and

lower

social

protection.

This

supports

the

Infrastructural

Medicine

proposition

that

built

environment

and

protection

architecture

can

modify

the

dose

and

persistence

of

a

biological

exposure.

It

does

not

mean

housing

alone

caused

each

death;

age,

chronic

disease,

medications,

social

isolation,

and

other

factors

also

modified

risk. 3A.4 Test C — Drinking-water infrastructure and exposure-state restoration Indigenous Services Canada reported 40 active long-term drinking-water advisories on public

systems

on

reserve

in

38

First

Nations

communities

as

of

August

13,

2026,

affecting

approximately

5,780

homes

and

344

community

buildings.

The

same

program

reported

156

long-term

advisories

lifted

since

November

2015.

Recent

advisories

were

lifted

after

concrete

infrastructural

changes

including

water-treatment-plant

upgrades,

watermain

replacement,

repairs,

improved

operations,

and

water-quality

monitoring

(Indigenous

Services

Canada,

2026a,

2026b). Result: strong support for the upstream infrastructure-to-exposure-control stage and for restoration

as

an

empirical

branch.

When

treatment,

distribution,

operations,

or

monitoring

are

repaired,

the

formal

exposure-risk

state

can

change.

These

administrative

data

do

not

directly

demonstrate

the

magnitude

of

downstream

disease

avoided

and

therefore

cannot

be

used

as

proof

of

a

specific

health

effect

without

health-outcome

linkage. 3A.5 Test D — Visibility lag The heat-dome investigation provides a bounded example of recognition and response lag:

the

Death

Review

Panel

reported

a

lag

between

alerts

issued

by

Environment

and

Climate

Change

Canada

and

public

agencies

and

effective

public

response

during

an

event

in

which

mortality

rose

rapidly.

In

drinking

water,

the

federal

long-term-advisory

category

itself

begins

only

after

an

advisory

has

persisted

for

more

than

12

months,

illustrating

how

administrative

categories

can

recognize

persistence

later

than

the

material

condition

began.

Result: descriptive support for the separability of material onset and institutional recognition.

The

stronger

proposition—that

visibility

lag

systematically

worsens

health

outcomes—requires

longitudinal

comparison

and

is

not

treated

as

established

by

these

cases

alone. 3A.6 Test E — Restoration versus deterministic deterioration The drinking-water evidence supplies a necessary counterexample to any deterministic collapse

narrative.

Infrastructure

can

be

repaired:

treatment

plants

can

be

expanded,

mains

replaced,

operations

improved,

advisories

lifted,

and

exposure-control

capacity

restored.

Infrastructural

Medicine

therefore

requires

a

restoration

branch

rather

than

a

one-way

erosion

model. Infrastructure Repair → Exposure Reduction/Control → Recovery Opportunity ↑ Result: the field's causal architecture is strengthened by including restoration as a falsifiable

outcome.

If

upstream

repair

changes

neither

exposure

nor

downstream

health

in

settings

where

the

mechanism

predicts

change,

the

proposed

pathway

is

weakened. 3A.7 Empirical decision table Proposition Current evidence Status Population health exposure can be materially structured upstream Air pollution exposure linked to large attributable mortality/morbidity burden Supported strongly Built environment can modify biological exposure and mortality 98% of 2021 BC heat deaths indoors; inadequate cooling common Supported strongly in event case Infrastructure restoration can alter exposure-control state Treatment/distribution/operations upgrades associated with lifting water advisories Supported upstream mechanism Recognition time can differ from material onset Heat-response lag; long-term water advisory defined only after 12 months Supported descriptively Infrastructure explains all or most disease No such evidence; biology, behaviour, chance, pathogens and care remain independent contributors Rejected Infrastructure variables outperform established public-health/exposome models No held-out comparative test yet Not established Slow Harm universally predicts later disease Current multi-case evidence is insufficient for universal temporal prediction Not established Restoration is possible Multiple long-term water advisories have been lifted after infrastructure/operations changes Supported 3A.8 Field status after empirical testing Infrastructural Medicine survives the field test in a narrower form than a universal structural

theory

of

illness.

Its

distinct

object

is

the

infrastructural

production,

routing,

persistence,

and

modification

of

health-relevant

exposure

before

and

around

clinical

manifestation.

Current

evidence

demonstrates

that

upstream

systems

can

distribute

exposure,

alter

protection

and

recovery,

generate

substantial

health

burden,

and

be

restored.

The strongest defensible classification is: empirically grounded emerging transdisciplinary

health

field.

The

current

evidence

does

not

establish

predictive

superiority

over

social

epidemiology,

environmental

health,

public

health,

or

exposomics,

and

it

does

not

validate

every

application

chapter.

Field

status

rests

on

the

common

exposure-architecture

object

and

present

mechanism

evidence;

stronger

uniqueness

depends

on

incremental-validity

tests. 3A.9 Falsification and discriminant validity The field should be narrowed or demoted if infrastructure source, routing, persistence, substitutability,

and

recovery

variables

cannot

be

measured

reliably;

if

they

add

no

explanatory

or

predictive

value

beyond

direct

exposure

and

standard

covariates;

if

infrastructural

interventions

fail

to

change

the

exposures

they

are

hypothesized

to

control;

or

if

downstream

health

changes

consistently

occur

independently

of

the

proposed

infrastructural

pathway.

Null,

restorative,

and

biologically

dominated

cases

are

part

of

the

empirical

architecture. 4. Slow Harm: The Temporal Architecture of Disease The SR Slow Harm tradition argues that injury can be real before it is institutionally

legible.

Repeated

low-intensity

exposures,

chronic

stressors,

nutritional

insufficiency,

administrative

delay,

and

interrupted

recovery

may

accumulate

without

producing

a

single

dramatic

event.

The

scientific

task

is

to

identify

dose,

duration,

reversibility,

and

biological

markers

rather

than

treating

graduality

as

proof

of

causation.

4.1 Cumulative Exposure What matters for cumulative exposure is not its rhetorical plausibility but its measurable

position

in

the

pathway.

The

field

treats

cumulative

exposure

as

a

pathway only when the exposure can be located, its recipients identified, its mechanism

defended,

and

its

downstream

signal

specified.

Those

quantities

should

enter

the

analysis

independently,

allowing

one

part

of

the

pathway

to

be

rejected

without

forcing

conclusions

about

the

rest.

The next analytical step is to determine whether the observed pattern survives alternative

explanations.

For

cumulative

exposure,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Interpretation here depends on separating the observed endpoint from the pathway

that

produced

it.

In

models

of

cumulative

exposure,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The claim becomes testable once exposure history and recovery conditions are

represented

separately.

A

study

of

cumulative

exposure

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

4.2 Latency Before latency can enter an SR model, its causal role has to be delimited. Research

on

latency

should

distinguish

the

condition

producing

the

exposure

from

the

people

receiving

it,

the

mechanism

carrying

it

forward,

and

the

endpoint

used

to

test

the

claim.

Operational

independence

keeps

the

field

from

treating

the

existence

of

an

institution

or

environment

as

proof

of

its

biological

effect.

The strongest evidence would show that altering the proposed upstream condition

changes

the

downstream

trajectory.

For

latency,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation window must also include plausible latency and recovery; otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

In

models

of

latency,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The observed outcome should be read alongside the conditions that made it more

or

less

likely.

A

study

of

latency

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

evidence

that

an

unobserved

must

4.3 Threshold Effects

The chapter treats threshold effects as a candidate mechanism, not as a conclusion.

The

threshold

effects

pathway

is

testable

only

when

its

source,

distribution,

mediator,

and

predicted

health

consequence

are

specified

separately.

This

separation

is

methodologically

important

because

structural

plausibility

is

not

itself

evidence

of

mediation.

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather than

being

compressed

into

one

explanation.

For

threshold

effects,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

In

models

of

threshold

effects,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection, reverse causation, and competing mechanisms therefore belong in the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The relevant causal question changes once timing and exposure history are made

explicit.

A

study

of

threshold

effects

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

4.4 Recovery Deficits Recovery Deficits is the point at which the chapter becomes specific. The empirical

question

for

recovery

deficits

is

whether

a

defined

upstream

change

produces

a

corresponding

exposure

shift,

a

plausible

intermediate

response,

and

the

predicted

health

outcome.

Each

component

should

be

measured

on

its

own

terms

so

that

a

broad

structural

category

cannot

stand

in

for

evidence.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

For

recovery

deficits,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed exposure where those distinctions are relevant. The observation window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

The pathway should be evaluated against both its predicted signal and its plausible

rivals.

In

models

of

recovery

deficits,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

respond

to

symptoms,

or

causes

Confounding,

and

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

A

study

of

recovery

deficits

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

bias

inference.

Absence

from

clinical

or

is

absence,

but

is

incomplete

evidence

that

an

4.5 Measurement Windows

For measurement windows, the causal claim begins with definition rather than

interpretation.

The

causal

chain

for

must

to

who

how

is

and

what

measurable

follows.

The

design

should

preserve

those

distinctions

from

the

outset;

otherwise

exposure,

mechanism,

and

outcome

collapse

same

assertion.

The strongest evidence would show that altering the proposed upstream condition

changes

the

downstream

trajectory.

For

measurement

windows,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

In

models

of

measurement

windows,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

Biological

predisposition

and protective factors can also dominate in particular cases. Confounding, selection,

The appropriate inference depends on the quality, timing, and granularity of the

study

document

which

are

Absence

from

4.6 Early Warning A useful analysis of early warning starts by locating the condition in an observable

chain.

Evaluation

of

early

warning

requires

four

linked

observations:

its

upstream

driver,

who

actually

encounters

it,

the

mechanism

through

which

it

could

matter,

and

the

outcome

expected

to

respond.

is

necessary

because

a

can

otherwise

conceal

a

weak

or

causal

early

should

distinguish

acute,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

and

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

This distinction matters because administrative records and causal processes do

not

necessarily

align.

of

action

may

an

susceptibility,

to

early

4.7 SR Formalization Risk(t) = f[Exposure(t), Susceptibility, Duration, Recovery, Care Access] SlowHarm(t) = ∫ ₀ ᵗ max(0, Load( τ) − Recovery( τ)) d τ VisibilityLag = T(recognition) − T(material change) The formal expressions in this chapter are conceptual measurement scaffolds rather

than

established

clinical

calculators.

They

state

what

would

need

to

be

measured

if

the

proposed

pathway

is

to

become

an

empirical

model.

Disease-specific

parameters,

compatible

scales,

validation

samples,

uncertainty

intervals,

and

sensitivity

analyses

are

required

before

numerical

interpretation.

4.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in cumulative exposure precede variation in the relevant

health

outcome?

•

Mechanistic plausibility is only the beginning; the proposed sequence must also

fit

observed

timing.

•

The next analytical step is to determine whether the observed pattern survives

alternative

explanations.

•

For causal purposes, the decisive issue is how the proposed pathway behaves

over

time.

•

For threshold effects, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning recovery deficits would falsify, reverse, or materially

narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

4.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on Slow Harm is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

5. The Harm Stack

The Harm Stack describes simultaneous burdens that interact across domains. Housing

insecurity

can

alter

sleep;

poor

sleep

can

alter

executive

function;

administrative

burden

can

consume

time

needed

for

care;

food

insecurity

can

narrow

diet;

chronic

stress

can

modify

physiological

regulation.

The

stack

is

a

hypothesis

about

interaction

and

accumulation,

not

permission

to

count

the

same

causal

effect

repeatedly.

5.1 Stacked Exposures The evidentiary problem posed by stacked exposures is concrete. Rather than treating

stacked

exposures

as

a

structural

label,

the

field

requires

a

source,

an

exposure

distribution,

a

credible

mediating

route,

and

a

prespecified

outcome.

Those

quantities

should

enter

the

analysis

independently,

allowing

one

part

of

the

pathway

to

be

rejected

without

forcing

conclusions

about

the

rest.

Mechanistic plausibility is only the beginning; the proposed sequence must also

fit

observed

timing.

For

stacked

exposures,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window must also include plausible latency and recovery; otherwise a cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

In

models

of

stacked

exposures,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

This relationship is best evaluated as a sequence rather than as a static association.

A

study

of

stacked

exposures

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

individual-level

5.4 Vulnerability The chapter treats vulnerability as a candidate mechanism, not as a conclusion.

The

causal

chain

for

vulnerability

must

be

explicit

enough

to

show

where

the

pressure

originates,

who

bears

it,

how

it

is

embodied,

and

what

measurable

consequence

follows.

No

single

composite

should

substitute

and

vulnerability,

investigators

distinguish

and

observation

a

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

and

individual

agency.

models

of

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

study

of

should

which

and

the

endpoint used to test the claim. Separate measures are required at each stage so

that

the

model

can

fail

at

a

specific

link

rather

than

survive

as

an

unfalsifiable

whole.

At this stage, measurement has to distinguish the visible outcome from the underlying

process.

For

buffer

depletion,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

can

both

and

magnitude.

This distinction matters because administrative records and causal processes do

not

necessarily

Confounding,

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

study

is

evidence

5.6 Cascade For cascade, the causal claim begins with definition rather than interpretation. Evaluation

of

cascade

requires

four

linked

its

upstream

driver,

who

actually

encounters

the

through

which

it

could

matter,

the

expected

to

The

pathway

remains

until

these

elements

can

be

observed

without

defining

one

by

reference

to

another.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

observation

window

must

also

include plausible latency and recovery; otherwise a cross-sectional snapshot can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather than

being

into

one

explanation.

models

of

action

SlowHarm(t) = ∫ ₀ ᵗ max(0, Load( τ) − Recovery( τ)) d τ VisibilityLag = T(recognition) − T(material change) These SR expressions are conceptual measurement scaffolds rather than established

5.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in stacked exposures precede variation in the relevant

health

outcome?

•

A useful design must preserve heterogeneity instead of treating variation as noise.

•

The pathway should be evaluated against both its predicted signal and its plausible

rivals.

•

This question cannot be resolved from prevalence alone; it requires pathway-specific

evidence.

•

For co-occurrence, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning vulnerability would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

5.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on Harm Stack is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

6. Food as Health Infrastructure Application status. Food systems are a test domain, not independent proof of the field. Affordability,

availability,

time,

marketing,

processing,

and

geography

must

be

linked

to

actual

intake

or

nutritional

exposure

before

downstream

biological

claims

are

made. Food is both a biological input and a logistical system. Diet is chosen, but choice

is

made

inside

price,

geography,

time,

culture,

marketing,

household

resources,

and

product

availability.

Infrastructural

Medicine

therefore

studies

not only what people eat but the architecture that makes some dietary patterns cheap,

convenient,

ubiquitous,

or

difficult

to

avoid.

6.1 Affordability Affordability cannot be inferred from the domain label alone. The field treats affordability

as

a

pathway

only

when

the

exposure

can

be

located,

its

recipients

identified,

its

mechanism

defended,

and

its

downstream

signal

specified.

No

single

composite

label

should

substitute

for

direct

evidence

about

the

source,

exposure,

mediator,

and

endpoint.

The endpoint can be clinically clear while its upstream architecture remains uncertain.

For

affordability,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

This distinction matters because administrative records and causal processes do

not

necessarily

align.

In

models

of

affordability,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms, or share causes with the outcome. Biological predisposition and protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

rather

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The model gains precision by separating what the system records from what the

actually

experiences.

A

study

of

document

which

variables

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

or

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

is

incomplete

evidence

is

claim

that

7. Water as Health Infrastructure Application status. Water is among the clearest infrastructure-to-exposure domains because

protection,

distribution,

and

are

system-level

Disease

still

require

contaminant-

source

or

field

rather

than

'water

quality'

as

a

single

treating

existence

of

an

institution

or

environment

as

proof

of

its

biological

effect.

This relationship is best evaluated as a sequence rather than as a static association.

For

treatment,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

In

models

of

treatment,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

and

dominate

Confounding,

and

mechanisms

belong

in

the

primary

rather

in

defensive

footnote.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

A

study

of

treatment

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

is

absence,

incomplete

evidence

an

in

defined

a

where

7.5 Monitoring

Before monitoring can enter an SR model, its causal role has to be delimited. The

field

treats

monitoring

as

a

pathway

only

when

the

exposure

can

be

its

recipients

identified,

its

mechanism

and

signal

specified.

No

single

composite

label

should

substitute

for

direct

evidence

about

the

source,

exposure,

mediator,

and

endpoint.

A longitudinal view is essential because the relevant load may precede diagnosis

by

years.

For

monitoring,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

plausible

and

otherwise

a

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

This relationship is best evaluated as a sequence rather than as a static association.

models

of

monitoring,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation, and competing mechanisms therefore belong in the primary analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The evidence becomes more informative when this relationship is specified directly.

A

study

of

monitoring

should

document

which

variables

are

are

what

is

and

Absence

from

is

but

is

incomplete

evidence

that

an

established

clinical

calculators.

7.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in source quality precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

•

The claim becomes testable once exposure history and recovery conditions are

represented

separately.

•

This part of the argument turns on temporal ordering and measurable mediation.

•

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

•

For distribution, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning contaminants would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

7.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on Water systems is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

8. Food–Water Cycles and Exposure Food and water systems are coupled. Agricultural inputs affect watersheds; water

quality

affects

food

production;

industrial

processing

changes

both

consumption

patterns

and

waste

streams.

The

relevant

unit

is

therefore

often

a cycle rather than a product. SR examines where exposure is introduced, diluted,

concentrated,

monitored,

or

externalized.

8.1 Agriculture For agriculture, the causal claim begins with definition rather than interpretation.

For

agriculture,

causal

resolution

depends

on

observing

the

proposed

source

and

mediator

rather

than

inferring

both

from

the

final

diagnosis.

Each

component

should

be

measured

on

its

own

terms

so

that

a

broad

structural

category

cannot

stand

in

for

evidence.

What matters here is not the existence of correlation alone but the structure that

would

generate

it.

For

agriculture,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

must

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

The empirical burden is therefore to identify what changed, when it changed, and

through

which

mechanism.

In

models

of

agriculture,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms, or share causes with the outcome. Biological predisposition and protective

factors

The next analytical step is to determine whether the observed pattern survives

8.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on Food–water coupling is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

9. Mental Health as Embodied Environment Application status. Mental-health outcomes are multicausal. Housing, income, social connection,

sleep,

administrative

burden,

and

care

access

are

treated

as

measurable

exposures

or

moderators,

not

as

sufficient

causes

of

psychiatric

illness. Mental health cannot be reduced either to brain chemistry or to social conditions.

Infrastructural

Medicine

treats

psychiatric

outcomes

as

emerging

from

interactions

among

neurobiology,

developmental

history,

social

stress,

material

conditions,

sleep,

substances,

nutrition,

and

access

to

care.

Structural

explanation

must

complement

rather

than

erase

individual

heterogeneity.

stress

load

becomes

and

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

that

can

be

compared

with alternatives, and changed. Where evidence supports that layer, medicine gains

an

Where

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

10. ADHD and Infrastructural Ecology Application status. ADHD is a neurodevelopmental condition with substantial heritable and

biological

components.

chapter

tests

environmental

and

how

environmental

conditions

modulate

expression.

refined

field

rejects

claims

that

nutritional

or

environmental

explain

ADHD

universally.

Instead

it

studies

moderators,

mimics,

comorbidities,

and

treatable

contributors

while

preserving

established

neurodevelopmental

evidence.

and

the

predicted

health

outcome.

Each

component

should

be

measured

on

its

own

terms

so

that

a

broad

structural

category

cannot

stand

in

for

evidence.

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather than

being

compressed

into

one

explanation.

For

nutrition,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise a cross-sectional snapshot can misrepresent both direction and magnitude.

The relevant causal question changes once timing and exposure history are made

explicit.

In

models

of

nutrition,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

respond

to

symptoms,

causes

outcome.

Biological

factors

and

mechanisms

in

footnote.

A credible estimate must account for both the accumulation of load and the possibility

of

study

nutrition

document

10.5 Environmental Modulation

Environmental Modulation cannot be inferred from the domain label alone. For

environmental

modulation,

rather

footnote.

through

which

mechanism.

document

from

10.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in neurodevelopment precede variation in the relevant

health

outcome?

•

A useful design must preserve heterogeneity instead of treating variation as noise.

•

This part of the argument turns on temporal ordering and measurable mediation.

•

The pathway should be evaluated against both its predicted signal and its plausible

rivals.

•

For trauma, which populations or settings show the longest delay between material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning nutrition would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

10.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on ADHD is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure' nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

11. Nutritional Psychiatry Application status. Diet-related pathways may modify mental-health outcomes in some settings,

but

nutritional

psychiatry

remains

an

established

neighboring

research

area.

Its

presence here is an application of exposure architecture, not a novelty claim by Infrastructural

Medicine. Nutritional psychiatry is relevant because metabolism, dietary quality, and nutrient

status

can

influence

neurobiological

function.

But

the

field

must

distinguish

evidence

for

adjunctive

benefit

from

claims

of

replacement

therapy.

Nutrient

deficiency

can

mimic

or

aggravate

symptoms

in

some

patients

without

implying

that

psychiatric

disorders

are

simply

nutritional

deficiencies.

of

through

the

expected

and

both

and

11.5 Trial Evidence A useful analysis of trial evidence starts by locating the condition in an observable

chain.

The

empirical

question

for

trial

evidence

is

whether

a

defined

upstream

change

produces

a

corresponding

exposure

shift,

a

components

separate

prevents

the

infrastructure

label

from

doing

explanatory

work

that

belongs

to

measurement.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

trial

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

where

observation

and

a

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

This relationship is best evaluated as a sequence rather than as a static association.

11.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in diet quality precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

This relationship is best evaluated as a sequence rather than as a static association.

The observed outcome should be read alongside the conditions that made it more

or

less

likely.

the

11.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on nutritional psychiatry is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

an

Where

12. Stress Physiology and the Pressure Body Application status. Allostatic load and stress physiology predate SR. The field-specific question

is

whether

identifiable

infrastructures

alter

the

persistence,

distribution,

or

recoverability

of

stress

exposure

beyond

individual-level

explanations. Chronic stress provides one of the clearest bridges between social conditions and

physiology.

The

SR

contribution

is

to

locate

recurring

stressors

in

systems:

unstable

work

schedules,

housing

insecurity,

debt,

caregiving

constraints, discrimination, unsafe environments, and administrative uncertainty.

The

body

is

not

outside

these

conditions;

it

adapts

to

them.

12.1 Allostatic Load What matters for allostatic load is not its rhetorical plausibility but its measurable

position

in

the

pathway.

Rather

than

treating

allostatic

load

as

a

structural

label,

the

field

requires

a

source,

an

exposure

distribution,

a

12.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in allostatic load precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

•

The relevant causal question changes once timing and exposure history are made

explicit.

•

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather

than

being

compressed

into

one

explanation.

•

A useful design must preserve heterogeneity instead of treating variation as noise.

•

For autonomic regulation, which populations or settings show the longest delay

between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning sleep would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow the proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

12.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on chronic stress is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

13. Cancer: From Infrastructural Claim to Causal Discipline Application status. Cancer is not treated as an infrastructural disease category. The chapter

is

retained

precisely

to

enforce

causal

discipline:

carcinogen

exposure,

occupational

or

environmental

routing,

screening,

diagnosis,

and

treatment

access

must

be

separated

from

genetics,

stochastic

processes,

and

tumor

biology. Cancer is not one disease and cannot responsibly be described as merely infrastructural

or

merely

metabolic.

The

SR

cancer

manuscript

is

strongest

when

it

identifies

plausible

upstream

pathways—known

carcinogenic

exposures,

diet-related

risk,

chronic

inflammation,

unequal

screening,

occupational

hazards,

and

water

contaminants—and

weakest

when

metaphor

is

allowed

to

outrun

oncology.

The

field

therefore

narrows

the

claim:

infrastructure

can

shape

cancer

risk,

detection,

treatment,

and

survivorship

through

multiple

established

and

testable

pathways.

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

In

models

of

inequality,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The relevant causal question changes once timing and exposure history are made

explicit.

A

study

of

inequality

should

document

which

variables

are

directly observed, which are proxies, what is missing, and how recognition delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

13.6 Screening For screening, the causal claim begins with definition rather than interpretation.

A

defensible

screening

connects

to

a

exposed

an

and

because

of

actually

experiences.

distinguish

acute,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

are

relevant.

The

must

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

This distinction matters because administrative records and causal processes do

not

necessarily

align.

of

may

13.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in genetics precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

•

What matters here is not the existence of correlation alone but the structure that

would

generate

it.

•

The model gains precision by separating what the system records from what the

subject

actually

experiences.

•

Mechanistic plausibility is only the beginning; the proposed sequence must also

fit

observed

timing.

•

For metabolism, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning carcinogens would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

13.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on cancer is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure' nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

14. Environmental Exposure and the Exposome Boundary status. Exposomics and environmental epidemiology are direct parent literatures.

Infrastructural

Medicine

is

distinct

only

where

it

models

the

systems

that

generate

and

route

exposome

components

and

demonstrates

added

value

beyond

exposure

measurement

alone. Exposomics offers a natural empirical partner for Infrastructural Medicine because

it

attempts

to

characterize

environmental

exposures

across

the

life

course.

SR

adds

institutional

topology:

who

controls

the

source,

who

measures

it,

who

bears

the

exposure,

how

long

recognition

takes,

and

what

repair

mechanisms

exist.

14.1 Air

Air cannot be inferred from the domain label alone. Rather than treating air as

a

structural

label,

the

field

requires

a

source,

an

exposure

distribution,

a

credible

mediating

route,

and

a

prespecified

outcome.

Separate

measures

are

required

at

each

stage

so

that

the

model

can

fail

at

a

specific

link

rather

than

survive

as

an

unfalsifiable

whole.

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather than

being

compressed

into

one

explanation.

For

air,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Interpretation here depends on separating the observed endpoint from the pathway

that

produced

it.

In

models

of

air,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation, and competing mechanisms therefore belong in the primary analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The pathway should be evaluated against both its predicted signal and its plausible

rivals.

A

study

of

air

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

14.4 Occupation Before occupation can enter an SR model, its causal role has to be delimited. The

empirical

for

a

a

and

by

years.

distinguish

acute,

and

both

and

magnitude.

mechanism.

14.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in air precede variation in the relevant health outcome?

•

A useful design must preserve heterogeneity instead of treating variation as noise.

A stronger analysis begins by isolating the mechanism at issue.

14.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on environmental exposure is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

15. Public Health Failure and Visibility Lag Application status. Public-health surveillance and response are studied as recognition architectures.

Failure

must

be

defined

against

an

explicit

capacity,

timing,

or

response

standard;

delayed

recognition

alone

does

not

prove

negligence

or

institutional

collapse.

Public health systems can fail even when individual programs function. Failure

may

occur

through

delayed

recognition,

fragmented

jurisdiction,

weak

surveillance,

underinvestment,

or

interventions

that

target

behavior

while

leaving

upstream

exposure

unchanged.

SR

calls

the

time

between

material

change

and

institutional

recognition

visibility

lag.

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

A defensible account requires the exposure, mediator, and outcome to be distinguished

empirically.

In

models

of

recognition,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection, reverse causation, and competing mechanisms therefore belong in the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

This question cannot be resolved from prevalence alone; it requires pathway-specific

evidence.

A

study

of

recognition

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

15.3 Thresholds The evidentiary problem posed by thresholds is concrete. Evidence concerning

thresholds

becomes

interpretable

or

and

substitute

for

about

and

explicit.

should

distinguish

and

developmentally

exposure

plausible

latency

and

otherwise

a

both

direction

and

magnitude.

15.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in surveillance precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

•

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

•

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

•

The strongest evidence would show that altering the proposed upstream condition

changes

the

downstream

trajectory.

•

For thresholds, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning data gaps would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

15.9 Chapter Conclusion

The SR position on public health is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

16. Healthcare as Pressure-Conversion Infrastructure Healthcare receives harms produced elsewhere and converts them into diagnoses,

procedures,

prescriptions,

waiting

lists,

referrals,

and

administrative

records.

When

capacity

is

constrained,

upstream

social

pressure

is

converted

into

downstream

clinical

pressure.

This

does

not

mean

clinicians

cause

the

original

burden;

it

means

healthcare

becomes

the

terminal

absorber

of

failures

distributed

across

other

systems.

16.1 Access For access, the causal claim begins with definition rather than interpretation. A

defensible

access

connects

a

upstream

to

mechanism,

at

so

the

model

can

fail

at

a

specific

rather

than

survive

as

an

unfalsifiable

whole.

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

16.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in access precede variation in the relevant health outcome?

•

Mechanistic plausibility is only the beginning; the proposed sequence must also

fit

observed

timing.

•

The analysis should therefore follow the pathway across exposure, embodiment,

detection,

and

response.

•

The next analytical step is to determine whether the observed pattern survives

alternative

explanations.

•

For triage, which populations or settings show the longest delay between material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning continuity would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

16.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on healthcare is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

17. Administrative Harm in Medicine Administrative processes are clinically relevant when they delay access, exhaust

patients,

fragment

continuity,

or

shift

scarce

time

from

care

to

navigation.

The

causal

question

is

measurable:

how

much

treatment

delay,

abandonment,

deterioration,

or

inequity

is

attributable

to

administrative

friction

after

controlling

for

clinical

need

and

capacity?

17.1 Forms Forms is the point at which the chapter becomes specific. The empirical question

for

forms

is

whether

a

defined

upstream

change

produces

a

corresponding

exposure

shift,

a

plausible

intermediate

response,

and

the

predicted

health

outcome.

No

single

composite

label

should

substitute

for

direct

evidence

about

the

source,

exposure,

mediator,

and

endpoint.

A longitudinal view is essential because the relevant load may precede diagnosis

by

years.

For

forms,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

The analysis is strengthened when competing pathways remain visible rather than

being

compressed

into

one

explanation.

In

models

of

forms,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection, reverse causation, and competing mechanisms therefore belong in the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

The relevant causal question changes once timing and exposure history are made

explicit.

A

study

of

forms

should

document

which

variables

are

directly

observed,

which

are

proxies,

what

is

missing,

and

how

recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

mechanism

must

exist.

nor

'individual

choice

is

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

the

18. Pharmacy, PBMs, and Pharmaceutical Access Medicines reach patients through a chain of manufacturers, wholesalers, payers,

benefit

managers,

pharmacies,

prescribers,

regulators,

and

information

systems.

Each

layer

can

alter

access.

Infrastructural

Medicine

studies the therapeutic effect of the drug and the access architecture that determines

whether

the

patient

can

obtain

it

consistently.

18.1 Pricing The chapter treats pricing as a candidate mechanism, not as a conclusion. The pricing

pathway

is

testable

only

when

its

source,

distribution,

mediator,

and

predicted

health

consequence

are

specified

separately.

Measurement

at

each

link

also

makes

competing

explanations

easier

to

compare

and

replication

easier

to

perform.

The evidence becomes more informative when this relationship is specified directly.

pricing,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

distinctions

relevant.

observation

window

plausible

both

and

into

explanation.

models

of

pricing,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early symptoms, or share causes with the outcome. Biological predisposition and

protective

factors

relevant.

The

observation

window

must

also

include

plausible

latency

and

recovery;

otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

Causal interpretation improves when the model states what evidence would weaken

the

proposed

mechanism.

In

models

of

coverage,

individual

action

may

mediate

an

upstream

condition,

modify

susceptibility,

respond

to

early

symptoms,

or

share

causes

with

the

outcome.

Biological

predisposition

and

protective

factors

can

also

dominate

in

particular

cases.

Confounding,

selection,

reverse

causation,

and

competing

mechanisms

therefore

belong

in

the

primary

analysis

rather

than

in

a

defensive

footnote.

What matters here is not the existence of correlation alone but the structure that

would

generate

it.

A

study

of

coverage

should

document

which

variables

are directly observed, which are proxies, what is missing, and how recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

Absence

from

a

clinical

or

administrative

record

is

not

evidence

of

absence,

but

neither

is

incomplete

measurement

evidence

that

an

unobserved

structural

mechanism

must

exist.

18.5 Dispensing Dispensing cannot be inferred from the domain label alone. A study of dispensing

should

trace

the

upstream

through

and

an

that

distinguish

and

exposure

where

18.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on pharmaceutical infrastructure is therefore neither 'everything

is

infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

19. Drug Shortages and Supply Fragility A medicine that exists scientifically but is unavailable operationally is not fully

available

medicine.

Shortages

expose

the

dependence

of

clinical

care

on

manufacturing

geography,

inventory

strategy,

procurement,

quality

systems,

and

substitution

capacity.

The

relevant

outcome

is

not

merely

shortage

count

but

patient-level

delay,

substitution,

error

risk,

and

outcome

change.

19.1 Manufacturing Concentration Before manufacturing concentration can enter an SR model, its causal role has

to

be

delimited.

The

field

treats

manufacturing

concentration

as

a

pathway

only

when

the

exposure

can

be

located,

its

recipients

identified,

its

mechanism

defended,

and

its

downstream

signal

specified.

Each

component

should

be

measured

on

its

own

terms

so

that

a

broad

structural

category

cannot

stand

in

for

evidence.

Population-level regularities become useful only when they can be connected to

a

plausible

individual-level

pathway.

For

manufacturing

concentration,

investigators

should

distinguish

acute,

repeated,

intermittent,

cumulative,

and

developmentally

timed

exposure

where

those

distinctions

are

relevant.

The

observation window must also include plausible latency and recovery; otherwise

a

cross-sectional

snapshot

can

misrepresent

both

direction

and

magnitude.

A useful design must preserve heterogeneity instead of treating variation as noise.

models

of

manufacturing

action

document

which

19.9 Chapter Conclusion

The SR position on drug supply is therefore neither 'everything is infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

20. The Industrialization of Medicine Industrialization has expanded medicine's technical power while also introducing

throughput

pressures,

concentration,

administrative

complexity,

and

dependence

on

large

supply

and

information

systems.

SR

treats

this

as

a

dual

reality:

industrial

capacity

can

save

lives

at

scale

and

can

also

create

new

systemic

failure

modes.

variables are directly observed, which are proxies, what is missing, and how recognition

delay

could

bias

inference.

from

or

evidence

20.2 Throughput Before throughput can enter an SR model, its causal role has to be delimited. Evaluation

of

throughput

requires

its

the

and

easier

distinguish

of

clinical

They

to

20.8 Research Questions •

Does measurable variation in specialization precede variation in the relevant health

outcome?

•

This question cannot be resolved from prevalence alone; it requires pathway-specific

evidence.

•

For causal purposes, the decisive issue is how the proposed pathway behaves

over

time.

•

The empirical burden is therefore to identify what changed, when it changed,

and

through

which

mechanism.

•

For technology, which populations or settings show the longest delay between

material

change

and

reliable

recognition?

•

What result concerning market structure would falsify, reverse, or materially narrow

the

proposed

infrastructural

interpretation?

20.9 Chapter Conclusion The SR position on medical industrialization is therefore neither 'everything is

infrastructure'

nor

'individual

choice

is

irrelevant.'

The

claim

is

that

infrastructure

is

a

causal

layer

that

can

be

specified,

measured,

compared

with

alternatives,

and

changed.

Where

evidence

supports

that

layer,

medicine

gains

an

upstream

target.

Where

evidence

does

not

support

it,

the

field

must

say

so.

21. Incentives and Health-System Behavior Institutions respond to incentives. Payment rules, performance targets, procurement

rules,

liability

structures,

staffing

models,

and

reporting

requirements

can

change

behavior

even

when

no

actor

intends

harm.

The

field therefore studies incentive topology rather than assuming either benevolence

or

conspiracy.
